# Round 33 of the relation-map review

Read the brief for round 32 at `audit_evidence_claims/m32/brief.md` (scope, guarantees, the approval bar) and its report at `audit_evidence_claims/m32/REPORT.md`. Repairs since then are in `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md`, in the sections "Round 32" and "pre-33". Review the latest commit on HEAD. Run tests with `python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` (expect 362). Start the page with `python src/app.py <port>` (not 8765).

## What changed since round 32

The map no longer compares the input with the linker's window. It looks the input up in its own index of stored names. Everything is in `src/app.py` `Engine.__init__` and `Engine.explore`, plus `_sig` and `_add_name`.

- **The comparison rule.** `_sig` drops trademark signs before NFKC, then applies casefold, strips accents, and drops categories P/Z/C. Every other letter (any script), digit and symbol is kept. Every name source is indexed by `_sig`.
- **Sources the map reads:**
  - a drug's ChEMBL preferred name and trade names
  - a small molecule's ChEMBL synonyms
  - every node's own label
  - target symbols
  - company and disease keys, matched only when `_sig(text)` equals the dictionary key (letters and digits only)
- **Sources the map does not read, each with its own reply:**
  - `registry_name`: synonyms found only in trial registrations (AACT). These are the sources of the round-32 data findings D1 to D3, and of 13-cis retinoic acid → tretinoin.
  - `biologic_synonym`: an antibody's or protein's ChEMBL synonyms. These mix the originator's codes with biosimilar codes and brands filed under the reference molecule (CT-P13 and Zessly under infliximab, ABP 980 under trastuzumab, the bevacizumab codes).
- **Choosing and refusing:**
  - All live candidates under one signature are collected. More than one gives `choose`; exactly one is drawn.
  - The linker still runs first, and any refused part refuses the whole input.
- **Brand list, in the question linker (`src/query.py`).** `_OTHER_PRODUCT_NAMES` holds trade names that ChEMBL files under a molecule although they name another product: European biosimilar brands and formulations such as Myocet, Fyarro, Megace ES, Jelmyto and Locteron. Both the map and the question boxes refuse them. The list was built by reviewing every biologic trade name in the dictionary; see the comment in the code.
- **Misfiled brand.** `_MISFILED` in `src/app.py`: Kadcyla is also filed under trastuzumab; the map reads it only as trastuzumab emtansine.
- **Question linker.** In a question, a key shared by several drugs is read as the drug whose own preferred name it is, letter for letter. This fixes the inherited "What trials evaluate NICOTINE?" case.
- **Docs:** README defects 9 and 15 (both languages) and section 0 of `web/try.html`.

## What to do

1. Re-run every round-32 finding: M32-B1 to B4, D1 to D3 and the should-fix items. Mark each FIXED or NOT FIXED with exact inputs. Also re-run the earlier historical replays (m1 to m31) against the map.
2. Attack the new design:
   - any input drawn for a node of which it is not, as a whole, one stored name under `_sig`
   - a stored name the map still reads (ChEMBL preferred name, trade name, small-molecule synonym, node label, target symbol, company or disease key) that denotes a different product than the node drawn
   - inconsistency between the name sources
   - Unicode, symbols, invisible characters
   - the `choose`, `registry_name` and `biologic_synonym` replies, including their markup safety
   - retired nodes
   - the id path
3. Check the question linker changes (`_OTHER_PRODUCT_NAMES`, the shared-key preferred-name rule) for regressions in the question boxes and the demo examples.
4. Re-check guarantees A to G briefly.

Report data problems (a stored name the map still reads, with a wrong identity) separately from code problems. Give the exact input, the source record (alias, source, alias_type) and an independent identity reference.

Recorded and not blockers:
- Leaks of free text in the question boxes (README defect 15).
- The resolver that builds the graph keeps its own brand lists, so a trial naming a biosimilar brand may be attached to the reference molecule in the graph (README defect 15).
- Known over-refusals: the list in `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md`, registry-only names, antibody development codes, and two tracer drugs refused by their own labels.

Write scratch files only under `audit_evidence_claims/m33/`. Put the report in `audit_evidence_claims/m33/REPORT.md`. Do not edit tracked files. End with exactly one line: `VERDICT: APPROVE` or `VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED`.

---
name: review
description: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes ??Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to "review since X".
---

Two-axis review of the diff between `HEAD` and a fixed point the user supplies:

- **Standards** ??does the code conform to this repo's documented coding standards?
- **Spec** ??does the code faithfully implement the originating issue / PRD / spec?

Both axes run as **parallel sub-agents** so they don't pollute each other's context, then this skill aggregates their findings.

The issue tracker should have been provided to you ??run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills` if `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` is missing.

## Process

### 1. Pin the fixed point

Whatever the user said is the fixed point ??a commit SHA, branch name, tag, `main`, `HEAD~5`, etc. If they didn't specify one, ask for it.

Capture the diff command once: `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` (three-dot, so the comparison is against the merge-base). Also note the list of commits via `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`.

Before going further, confirm the fixed point resolves (`git rev-parse <fixed-point>`) and the diff is non-empty. A bad ref or empty diff should fail here ??not inside two parallel sub-agents.

### 2. Identify the spec source

Look for the originating spec, in this order:

1. Issue references in the commit messages (`#123`, `Closes #45`, GitLab `!67`, etc.) ??fetch via the workflow in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.
2. A path the user passed as an argument.
3. A PRD/spec file under `docs/`, `specs/`, or `.scratch/` matching the branch name or feature.
4. If nothing is found, ask the user where the spec is. If they say there isn't one, the **Spec** sub-agent will skip and report "no spec available".

### 3. Identify the standards sources

Anything in the repo that documents how code should be written, such as `CODING_STANDARDS.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md`.

### 4. Spawn both sub-agents in parallel

Send a single message with two `Agent` tool calls. Use the `general-purpose` subagent for both.

**Standards sub-agent prompt** ??include:

- The full diff command and commit list.
- The list of standards-source files you found in step 3.
- The brief: "Report ??per file/hunk where relevant ??every place the diff violates a documented standard. Cite the standard (file + the rule). Distinguish hard violations from judgement calls. Skip anything tooling enforces. Under 400 words."

**Spec sub-agent prompt** ??include:

- The diff command and commit list.
- The path or fetched contents of the spec.
- The brief: "Report: (a) requirements the spec asked for that are missing or partial; (b) behaviour in the diff that wasn't asked for (scope creep); (c) requirements that look implemented but where the implementation looks wrong. Quote the spec line for each finding. Under 400 words."

If the spec is missing, skip the Spec sub-agent and note this in the final report.

### 5. Aggregate

Present the two reports under `## Standards` and `## Spec` headings, verbatim or lightly cleaned. Do **not** merge or rerank findings ??the two axes are deliberately separate (see _Why two axes_).

End with a one-line summary: total findings per axis, and the worst issue _within each axis_ (if any). Don't pick a single winner across axes ??that's the reranking the separation exists to prevent.

## Why two axes

A change can pass one axis and fail the other:

- Code that follows every standard but implements the wrong thing ??**Standards pass, Spec fail.**
- Code that does exactly what the issue asked but breaks the project's conventions ??**Spec pass, Standards fail.**

Reporting them separately stops one axis from masking the other.

# Computer Use

Use this skill to automate the UI of Microsoft Windows apps. It uses SendInput, UI Automation, and Windows.Graphics.Capture screenshots that work even when windows are occluded.

If this plugin is available, read this entire `SKILL.md` once before Windows automation work, before saying Computer Use is unavailable, and before falling back to other Windows automation.

Start with the directions in the Initialize section below. Read these bundled Markdown files relative to this `SKILL.md` when you need information about the specific topic they cover:

- `../../docs/guidance.md`: core runtime behavior, target-window workflow, screenshot handling, and recovery guidance. You MUST read this before controlling Windows apps.
- `../../docs/api.md`: full `sky` API reference. Read this when you need method signatures or object shapes.
- `../../docs/confirmations.md`: you MUST read this before deciding whether a Windows UI action needs confirmation

## Initialize

The bundled `cua_node` `@oai/sky` package is the core entry point for Computer Use. Import it directly by package name from the JavaScript session. Do not spawn `codex-computer-use.exe`, search for the helper executable, or build a custom helper protocol client.

Run this once per fresh `node_repl` JavaScript session:

```js
if (!globalThis.sky) {
  const { sky } = await import("@oai/sky");
  globalThis.sky = sky;
}
```

# Round 32 of the relation-map review

Previous rounds: `audit_evidence_claims/m1` to `m31` (`REPORT.md`; m27 and m29 stopped early, so their `attacks/` folders are the record). All repairs are in `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md`. Review the latest commit on HEAD. Run the tests with `python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider`; expect 361. Start the page with `python src/app.py <port>` on a port other than 8765 and exercise it over HTTP.

## What changed since round 31: a design change, not another word-list repair

Rounds 20 to 31 each found new wording that the question linker's product markers did not cover. The relation map no longer depends on those markers. See the section "Before round 32" in `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md`, and `src/app.py` `Engine.explore`, `_sig`, `_latin`.

- **P32-1.** `explore` draws only when the whole input is one name. The linker must read exactly one entity, and its matched text must have the same letters, digits and symbols as the input (`_sig`: NFKC, casefold, any script). Case, spaces, punctuation and 짰/??짤 do not count. Anything else returns `not_one_name`, and the page asks for the name alone.
- **P32-2.** If any part of the input is refused, the whole input is refused.
- **P32-3.** A name stored for several live nodes is not drawn. The reply is `choose`: the nodes are listed and the reader clicks one, which loads it by id.
- **P32-4.** Lower-case `nct`/`chembl`/`ensg` ids are read. The whole input can be a company key or target name when `_latin(text)` equals the dictionary key, so a symbol the key would drop still blocks it (for example "Merck & Co").
- **P32-5.** This change is in the question linker, `src/query.py` `_whole_name` and the end of `_link`. A canonical key shared by several drugs is no longer decided by its spelling; only a drug's own preferred name chooses it. Inside such a key, a shorter name is not read either. Before, "medi4736" read as atezolizumab and "5-fluorouracil" as capecitabine.
- **New tests:** `tests/test_app.py::test_the_map_draws_only_a_whole_stored_name` and `test_a_key_shared_by_several_drugs_is_not_decided_by_spelling`. Older map tests that drew a centre from a longer input now go through `_page`: the map must not draw, and the question linker's reading is checked instead.
- **Docs:** README defect 15 (both languages) records that the question boxes still rely on the linker's markers for free text. Defect 9 records the shared keys. Section 0 of `web/try.html` states the one-name rule.

## Scope and approval bar

The subject is the relation map: section 0 of `web/try.html`, `POST /api/explore`, `Engine.explore`. The guarantees are those in the original brief below, with guarantee 3 now read as follows: the map draws a node only for an input that is, as a whole, one stored name of that node (or its id). It never draws a refused product, tracer or conjugate as the originator, and never draws one of several nodes that share a name.

1. Re-run every earlier reproduction from m1 to m31 against the map, and mark each finding FIXED or NOT FIXED. Reuse the earlier scripts. Inputs that were longer than a name should now be refused, `not_one_name`, `choose` or `not_found`, never a drawn originator.
2. Attack the new rule:
   - any input that draws a node the input does not name as a whole
   - a whole stored name that is in fact a different product (a data problem; say so, and separate it from code problems)
   - Unicode, symbols and invisible characters that `_sig`/`_latin` mishandle
   - the id path
   - the `choose` reply (live nodes only, injection-safe page markup, click-through)
   - company/target whole-name reads
   - retired nodes
   - anything the page text claims that the code does not do
3. Check P32-5 for regressions in the question boxes (2 to 4) and the demo examples. Report over-refusals as should-fix, not blockers.
4. Re-check guarantees A to G of the original brief briefly; earlier rounds approved them.

### Out of scope as blockers (recorded)

- The question boxes' free-text refusal relies on word lists (README defect 15). A leak there is a note, not a map blocker.
- Known data limitations, awaiting the owner's curation decision:
  - ChEMBL lists bevacizumab biosimilar development codes as bevacizumab synonyms.
  - An AACT synonym maps 13-cis-retinoic acid to CHEMBL38 tretinoin (M23-D1).
  - "Roche" is stored as a folic acid alias. The map now asks the reader to choose.
- Known over-refusals listed in `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md` (M5-S2, rapamycin forms, Trastuzumab--vcmmae conjugate, 9-amino-20(s)-camptothecin, il-6ra, TAK-659F, GS-11CAL-101, 3en3hg4wsw, an ADC named with its own payload beside it). A longer input not being drawn is the new rule, not an over-refusal.

Approval bar: no known way for the map to draw a node for an input that is not, as a whole, one stored name or id of it. That includes a refused product drawn as the originator, one of several same-named nodes drawn without the reader's choice, a wrong relation or direction, a value that differs from the stored one, and script injection. Report should-fix items separately from blockers.

Write scratch files only under `audit_evidence_claims/m32/`. Put the report in `audit_evidence_claims/m32/REPORT.md`. Do not edit tracked files. End with exactly one line: `VERDICT: APPROVE` or `VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED`.

# Independent relation-map review ??round 32

Reviewed **HEAD `b21da2b29c3333af2075433043e32eeb2bb8af21`**, parent `4493fe0f21ad7615594be5d7715b1b90de2a1ed6`, on 2026-11-04. **CHANGES REQUIRED.** The test suite passes, and the new design prevents the historical longer-input leaks. However, the map can still draw a node for an input that is not a whole stored name, or silently choose one of several live same-name nodes. New exact stored aliases also map different products to the wrong molecule; these are reported separately as data defects.

## Verification and limits

- Ran the requested `python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider`: **361 passed**. The first run took 48.16s and emitted one Windows cp949 subprocess-reader warning. A justified clean rerun with `PYTHONUTF8=1`, bytecode disabled and pytest/temp paths under m32 produced **361 passed in 43.71s, no warnings**. [Clean output](pytest_utf8.txt), [initial output](pytest.txt). The initial pytest invocation used pytest's default temporary location; subsequent audit scripts, artifacts and final-run temporary files are under m32.
- Started the actual application with **`python src/app.py 8932`** and exercised it over HTTP. The served page exactly equalled `web/try.html`. Root independently captured **50 requests**, including the principal code/data counterexamples, valid ids, refusals and input errors; other lanes retain their own original-CLI captures. [First30](root_http.json), [additional20 plus stored/live-name checks](root_additional_http.json). HTTP200 alone was never treated as semantic success.
- Compared **all 6,469 live centers** against independent SQLite results: **zero errors**, covering 22 relation/direction/type/role combinations. **19 DB assertions and 26 UI/server assertions pass**. All 36 demo HTTP responses equal direct Engine results and the query-only HEAD^ comparison.
- UI checks execute the actual served JavaScript in a **Node VM DOM/fetch harness**, using real HTTP for choices and demos. They verify generated markup, delegated clicks and state transitions; they are **not a painted-browser or visual-layout test**.
- No production code was changed and no model was called. Read paths and `use_llm=False` were inspected; private data mutations use in-memory copies. Final tracked status is clean; all 67 files in the root HTTP baseline, including the database/dictionary, remain byte-identical. That baseline was taken after the initial test run, not before it. The owned port 8932 is closed. [Root HTTP hash check](root_confirmation.json), [final integrity](integrity_final.json), [diff under review](review_diff.txt). The conditional KOA Studio/KhOpenApi before-and-after code requirement is not triggered by this read-only review.

## Code blockers

**M32-B1 ??comparing an echoed linker window does not prove a stored-name match.** At `src/app.py:111-115`, `_sig(input)` is compared with `_sig(matched_text)`. But `src/query.py:872-877` supplies the candidate input window after looking it up through the lossy canonical key (`src/textrules.py:340-347`). This proves input-span coverage, not equality to an actual stored alias. `_plain` permits characters the dictionary discards.

`trastu+zumab`, `trastu$zumab`, and `trastu=zumab` all draw **CHEMBL1201585 TRASTUZUMAB**. So do `trastuzumab횩`, `trastuzumab첩`, `trastuzumab챈`, `trastuzumab흢`, `trastuzumab챨`, and `trastuzumab캇`. The significant extra symbol/letter is absent from every stored alias of the drawn drug. The root independently checked full stored aliases/live nodes for `trastu+zumab` and `trastuzumab횩`; neither has a matching name. This violates P32-1 regardless of whether the altered text denotes a real marketed product. [HTTP and alias proof](root_additional_http.json), [broader variants](attacks/http_identity.json), [independent sanity assertions](attacks/sanity_assertions.json).

**M32-B2 ??company fallback bypasses significant-name equality.** `src/app.py:120-122` accepts a canonical company key whenever `_plain(text)` is true. It never compares the input with a stored name/key retaining significant symbols. **`AstraZeneca +` -> ORG:astrazeneca**, **`Merck + Co` -> ORG:merckco**, and **`Pfizer$`/`Pfi+zer` -> ORG:pfizer**. `Roche $` draws only the company, whereas `Roche` correctly offers the folic-acid/company choice. This is an independent acceptance path even when the linker-window equality fails. The requested `_latin` implementation is absent from actual HEAD; the reviewed implementation is `_plain`. `Merck & Co` itself is a valid stored-label control, not a reported counterexample. [Independent HTTP](root_http.json), [extra company controls](attacks/http_identity.json).

**M32-B3 ??shared-name collection uses inconsistent equivalence rules.** P32-3 requires a reader choice for several live nodes sharing one name, ignoring case/spacing/punctuation. The map's `_label_key` retains punctuation, target fallback is literal lowercase, and drug candidates inherit P32-5's question-only preferred-name selection (`src/app.py:111-125,242-244`; `src/query.py:742-747`). A sweep of155 ambiguous spellings finds six that draw rather than choose:

| Exact input | Live candidates sharing the significant name | Incorrect automatic result |
|---|---|---|
| `ABT-510` / `ABT 510` | CHEMBL386115 and CHEMBL262489 | Each spelling draws its own preferred-name node |
| `MAGE-A3` | CHEMBL2108705 ASTUPROTIMUT-R and ENSG00000221867 MAGEA3 | Draws the drug; `MAGEA3` correctly chooses |
| `ctla-4` | CHEMBL1789844 IPILIMUMAB and ENSG00000163599 CTLA4 | Draws the drug; `CTLA4` correctly chooses |
| `FLUDEOXYGLUCOSE F 18` / `FLUDEOXYGLUCOSE-F18` | CHEMBL2030434 and CHEMBL497613 | Each spelling draws its own preferred-name node |

All candidates were independently checked live in SQL. MAGE-A3 and ctla-4 demonstrate the failure without relying on the drug-preferred-name exception. Duplicate source names are data facts; automatically selecting one is a map-code violation. P32-5 allows preferred-name selection in question linking, but does not waive the map's choice rule. [Complete sweep](attacks/shared_signatures.json), [independent HTTP/SQL confirmation](root_additional_http.json).

**M32-B4 ??a supposedly ignored trademark changes identity.** NFKC expands `?? into letters `TM` before `_sig` removes trademark characters (`src/app.py:237-239`). **`Tarceva`/`Tarceva짰` draw CHEMBL1079742 ERLOTINIB HYDROCHLORIDE; `Tarceva?? draws CHEMBL553 ERLOTINIB.** The latter uses a separate stored AACT alias `tarcevatm`. That alias explains the lookup but cannot authorize interpreting a literal ignored trademark as significant letters under P32-1. `trastuzumab?? and `AstraZeneca?? instead lose recall; those are should-fix manifestations, not additional wrong-node cases. [Root HTTP](root_http.json), [interpretation checks](attacks/sanity_assertions.json).

A repair needs to establish identity against actual stored names, collect **all live candidates under one consistent name-equivalence rule**, and only then draw or choose. Comparing text with its own extracted window or checking a character class cannot establish this. Trademark handling must precede compatibility expansion. These are review conclusions, not an implemented patch.

## Data blockers, separate from code

The following are **exact stored AACT aliases** in `data/processed/resolved.json:1`, JSON path `dictionary.index[canonical_key(input)]`. The new map accepts the whole alias faithfully, but its source assignment identifies the wrong substance/product. These are outside the explicitly waived bevacizumab biosimilar codes, 13-cis-retinoic-acid and Roche limitations.

| Finding | Exact input and observed center | Independent identity evidence |
|---|---|---|
| M32-D1 | `bupivacaine hydrochloride` -> **ANTIMONY / CHEMBL4297652** | NCI identifies an amide local anesthetic, not antimony. [NCI definition](https://www.cancer.gov/publications/dictionaries/cancer-drug/def/bupivacaine-hydrochloride). |
| M32-D2 | `irinotecan liposome`, `irinotecan liposome injection`, `nanoliposomal irinotecan` -> **IRINOTECAN / CHEMBL481**; `doxorubicin hydrochloride liposome` -> **ordinary hydrochloride / CHEMBL359744** | These denote liposomal formulations. The Onivyde label explicitly distinguishes it from other irinotecan products; NCI separately identifies liposomal doxorubicin. [Manufacturer label](https://dailymed.nlm.nih.gov/dailymed/fda/fdaDrugXsl.cfm?setid=c8b58efa-1820-48a4-b70d-62918fc4abfc), [NCI formulation](https://www.cancer.gov/about-cancer/treatment/drugs/doxorubicin-hydrochloride-liposome). |
| M32-D3 | `ropeginterferon alfa-2b` -> **INTERFERON ALFA-2B / CHEMBL1201558** | NCI describes a PEG conjugate, rather than unconjugated interferon alfa-2b. [NCI definition](https://www.cancer.gov/publications/dictionaries/cancer-drug/def/ropeginterferon-alfa-2b). |

Root independently reproduced every table row over HTTP and retained the matching source records and live center. `Onivyde`, `liposomal irinotecan`, `Doxil`, and `liposomal doxorubicin` are refusal controls, showing that another stored spelling of a distinct formulation is drawn. [Root captures](root_additional_http.json), [source records and controls](attacks/http_data_aliases.json), [full analysis](attacks/FINDINGS.md). The 317 candidate aliases in the exploratory screen are **not 317 proven defects**. These selected rows need source curation; another free-text word-list patch would not establish source identity.

## Historical cost ledger

# Historical cost reproductions ??M32

Fourteen copied suite executions plus the reused isotope-instrumentation supplement exited0. The original2,000-piece answer was additionally exercised against the root CLI. Each reproduction and its disposition are below. Timings overlapped other audit work, so these are descriptive latencies, not an isolated benchmark or evidence of a new regression. Historical `isolated_` filenames do not imply process/host isolation. No timeout or wrong map is established by these performance checks; residual costs are should-fix items.

The implementation still runs the linker before the one-name test. Inputs over500 characters are tested as the question answer field or directly, not as successful map inputs. Shared API locking means slow question checks can delay valid maps.

|---|---|---|

"C:\\WINDOWS\\System32\\WindowsPowerShell\\v1.0\\powershell.exe" -Command '$env:PYTHONIOENCODING='"'utf-8'; Get-ChildItem -Path . -Filter AGENTS.md -Recurse -File | Select-Object -ExpandProperty FullName; Get-ChildItem audit_evidence_claims/m33 -Recurse -File | Select-Object FullName,Length; Get-Content -Encoding UTF8 audit_evidence_claims/m1/brief.md; Select-String -Path CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md -Pattern 'Round 32','pre-33' -Context 3,70; git diff --stat HEAD"'^ HEAD; git rev-parse HEAD''^; Select-String -Path C:/Users/geoum/.codex/memories/MEMORY.md -Pattern '"'oncokg','relation.map','M32','bio.?research'" in C:\Users\geoum\Dropbox\GY's Formal Workspace\2026 Job recruting\바이오리서치에이아이\oncokg

 exited 1 in 380ms:
"""A local page for trying the pipeline by hand.

    python src/app.py            then open http://localhost:8765

A relation map (a node and the nodes live edges tie it to, a capped list per relation,
click to move on),
then four boxes: type an intervention string the way a registry would write it and see
how the resolver reads it, or ask a question and see the grounded answer with its
evidence, give a structured answer (claims citing evidence edges) and see which
claims the graph supports and the answer written from them, or paste free prose and
see what the auxiliary word-order check flags. All of them call
the functions the pipeline uses (resolve_drug_string, OncoKG.answer,
OncoKG.check_groundedness) on the built artefacts, so what the page shows is what the
pipeline would do. Nothing is written; the language model is never called.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import json
import re
import sqlite3
import sys
import threading
import unicodedata
from http.server import BaseHTTPRequestHandler, ThreadingHTTPServer
from pathlib import Path

sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent))
import config as C
import normalize as N
import textrules as T
from query import OncoKG

PAGE = C.WEB / "try.html"
PORT = 8765


class Engine:
    def __init__(self):
        self.kg = OncoKG()
        # A browser opens spare connections it may never use, which would stall a
        # single-threaded server; requests are threaded, the engine is not.
        self.kg.conn.close()
        self.kg.conn = sqlite3.connect(C.PROCESSED / "oncokg.sqlite", check_same_thread=False)
        self.kg.conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
        self.lock = threading.Lock()
        self.dic = self.kg.dic
        self.index_keys = self.kg.index_keys
        target_rows, _, _ = N._load("targets")
        self.target_keys = N.target_guard_keys(target_rows, self.dic)
        # The names the relation map reads, under one rule (`_sig`): a drug's ChEMBL
        # preferred name, synonyms and trade names, every node's own label, and target
        # symbols. Synonyms copied from trial registrations are left out: they are where
        # the dictionary's wrong identities come from ("bupivacaine hydrochloride" filed
        # under antimony, "irinotecan liposome" under irinotecan); the question boxes
        # still read them.
        # An antibody's or protein's ChEMBL synonyms mix the originator's development
        # codes with biosimilars filed under the reference molecule (CT-P13 and Zessly
        # under infliximab, ABP 980 under trastuzumab, Jubbonti under denosumab), which
        # cannot be told apart; such a drug is read by its preferred name and trade names.
        self.names: dict = {}
        self.registry_names: dict = {}
        self.biologic_synonyms: dict = {}
        for hits in self.dic["index"].values():
            for h in hits:
                if (h["alias"], h["drug_id"]) in _MISFILED:
                    continue
                kind = (self.dic["drugs"].get(h["drug_id"]) or {}).get("drug_type")
                if h.get("source") == "AACT":
                    into = self.registry_names
                elif kind != "Small molecule" and h.get("alias_type") == "synonym":
                    into = self.biologic_synonyms
                else:
                    into = self.names
                _add_name(into, h["alias"], h["drug_id"])
        for r in self.kg.conn.execute("SELECT id, label FROM node"):
            _add_name(self.names, r["label"], r["id"])
        for t in self.kg.targets.values():
            _add_name(self.names, t["symbol"], t["target_id"])
        # names stored only as dictionary keys (letters and digits): company ids and
        # former names, the disease's synonyms
        self.keyed: dict = {}
        for k, ids in self.kg.companies.items():
            self.keyed.setdefault(k, []).extend(ids)
        for k in self.kg.disease["index"]:
            self.keyed.setdefault(k, []).append(self.kg.disease["disease_id"])

    def resolve(self, text: str) -> dict:
        out = N.resolve_drug_string(text, self.dic, self.index_keys, self.target_keys)
        for c in out["components"]:
            d = self.dic["drugs"].get(c.get("drug_id") or "")
            if d:
                c["drug"] = {k: d.get(k) for k in
                             ("name", "drug_type", "max_phase_overall", "max_phase_in_nsclc")}
                c["drug"]["mechanisms"] = [m.get("mechanismOfAction") if isinstance(m, dict) else m
                                           for m in (d.get("mechanisms") or [])][:3]
                c["drug"]["trials"] = self.kg.conn.execute(
                    "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM edge WHERE rel='EVALUATES' AND dst=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
                    (d["drug_id"],)).fetchone()[0]
                par = self.dic["drugs"].get(d.get("parent_molecule") or "")
                if par:
                    c["drug"]["parent"] = {"name": par["name"], "trials": self.kg.conn.execute(
                        "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM edge WHERE rel='EVALUATES' AND dst=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
                        (par["drug_id"],)).fetchone()[0]}
            if c.get("candidates"):
                c["candidate_names"] = [
                    (self.dic["drugs"].get(x) or {}).get("name", x) for x in c["candidates"]]
        return out

    def explore(self, text: str, per_group: int = 30) -> dict:
        """One live node and the live nodes its live edges tie it to, grouped by relation.

        `text` is a node id or one stored name, compared under one rule (`_sig`: case,
        accents, spaces, punctuation and trademark signs aside; every other letter,
        digit and symbol must match): a drug's ChEMBL preferred name or trade name, a
        small molecule's ChEMBL synonym, a node's own label, a target symbol, or a company
        or disease key. Anything more (a second name, a qualifier, a payload, a dye, a
        sentence) matches no stored name and is not drawn; the reply says to enter the
        name alone. A name the question linker refuses (a biosimilar, a reformulation, a
        tracer) is refused, whatever else the input names. Two kinds of stored synonym
        are not read, and the reply says which: one found only in trial registrations
        (they hold wrong identities), and an antibody's or protein's ChEMBL synonym (they
        include biosimilar codes). A name stored for several live nodes is not drawn
        either: they are listed and the reader picks one by id.

        Each group keeps `per_group` neighbours, sorted as the page states: trials by
        start date (latest first), drugs by trials in the graph, sponsors by trials they
        lead, disease targets by association score. Sponsorship keeps its role (lead
        sponsor or collaborator)."""
        conn = self.kg.conn
        matched = None
        ref = text.strip()
        if re.fullmatch(r"(?i)(nct|chembl|ensg)\d+", ref):
            ref = ref.upper()                         # an id typed in lower case
        row = conn.execute("SELECT id FROM node WHERE id=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
                           (ref,)).fetchone()
        if row:
            nid = row["id"]
        else:
            ents = self.kg.link_entities(text)
            refused = [e for e in ents if e["type"] == "Refused"]
            hits = [e for e in ents if e.get("id") and e["type"] != "Refused"]
            if refused:                       # any refused part: the input is not one plain name
                return {"refused": refused[0]["label"], "why": refused[0]["why"]}
            sig = _sig(text)
            found = dict(self.names.get(sig, {}))
            if sig and sig == T.canonical_key(text):          # nothing the key would drop
                for k in self.keyed.get(sig, []):
                    found.setdefault(k, None)
            ids = list(found)
            choices = []
            for oid in ids:
                lab = conn.execute("SELECT type, label FROM node WHERE id=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
                                   (oid,)).fetchone()
                if lab and all(c["id"] != oid for c in choices):
                    choices.append({"id": oid, "type": lab["type"], "label": lab["label"]})
            if not choices:
                if found:                     # stored, but its node is retired
                    return {"not_found": text}
                if self.biologic_synonyms.get(sig):
                    return {"biologic_synonym": text}
                if self.registry_names.get(sig):
                    return {"registry_name": text}   # a name only a trial registration gives
                return {"not_one_name": text} if hits else {"not_found": text}
            if len(choices) > 1:
                # one name, several nodes ("Roche"): none is drawn in place of the others;
                # the reader picks one by its id
                return {"choose": choices, "text": text}
            nid = choices[0]["id"]
            matched = found.get(nid)
        node = conn.execute("SELECT id, type, label, props FROM node WHERE id=? "
                            "AND retired_at IS NULL", (nid,)).fetchone()
        if node is None:                              # a name can outlive its node
            return {"not_found": text}
        rows = conn.execute(
            "SELECT e.id AS eid, e.rel, e.src, e.props AS eprops, n.id, n.type, n.label, "
            "n.props AS nprops FROM edge e JOIN node n "
            "ON n.id = CASE WHEN e.src=? THEN e.dst ELSE e.src END "
            "WHERE (e.src=? OR e.dst=?) AND e.retired_at IS NULL AND n.retired_at IS NULL",
            (nid, nid, nid)).fetchall()
        drug_ids = list({r["id"] for r in rows if r["type"] == "Drug"} | {nid})
        trials = {}
        for i in range(0, len(drug_ids), 500):
            chunk = drug_ids[i:i + 500]
            trials.update(conn.execute(               # live trials only
                "SELECT e.dst, COUNT(*) FROM edge e JOIN node t ON t.id = e.src "
                "WHERE e.rel='EVALUATES' AND e.retired_at IS NULL AND t.retired_at IS NULL "
                f"AND e.dst IN ({','.join('?' * len(chunk))}) GROUP BY e.dst", chunk).fetchall())
        groups: dict = {}
        for r in rows:
            ep, np_ = _obj(r["eprops"]), _obj(r["nprops"])
            item = {"id": r["id"], "type": r["type"], "label": r["label"], "edge_id": r["eid"]}
            role = ep.get("role") if r["rel"] == "SPONSORS" else None
            if r["type"] == "Drug":
                item["trials"] = trials.get(r["id"], 0)
                item["max_phase"] = np_.get("max_phase_overall")
            if r["rel"] == "SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF":
                n = ep.get("evidence_trials")
                # a count the edge does not record stays unknown; 0 is only for sorting
                item["evidence_trials"] = n if _number(n) else None
                item["weight"] = n if _number(n) else 0
                item["sponsor_class"] = ep.get("sponsor_class")
            elif r["type"] == "Trial":
                item.update({k: np_.get(k) for k in ("phases", "status", "title", "start_date", "url")})
                item["weight"] = str(np_.get("start_date") or "")
            elif r["type"] == "Drug":
                item["weight"] = item["trials"]
            elif r["rel"] == "ASSOCIATED_WITH":
                score = ep.get("association_score")
                item["score"] = score if _number(score) else None
                item["weight"] = item["score"] or 0
            else:
                item["weight"] = 0
            if r["rel"] == "ACTS_ON":
                item["action"] = ep.get("action_type")
            if role:
                item["role"] = role
            key = (r["rel"], "out" if r["src"] == nid else "in", r["type"], role)
            groups.setdefault(key, []).append(item)
        out = []
        for (rel, direction, ntype, role), items in groups.items():
            items.sort(key=lambda x: x["label"] or "")              # names A to Z
            items.sort(key=lambda x: x["weight"], reverse=True)     # then by the stated weight
            out.append({"rel": rel, "dir": direction, "type": ntype, "role": role,
                        "total": len(items), "items": items[:per_group]})
        out.sort(key=lambda g: -g["total"])
        props = _obj(node["props"])
        return {"center": {"id": node["id"], "type": node["type"], "label": node["label"],
                           "matched_text": matched,
                           "trials": trials.get(node["id"], 0) if node["type"] == "Drug" else None,
                           "props": {k: props.get(k) for k in
                                     ("max_phase_overall", "drug_type", "phases", "status",
                                      "title", "url", "start_date") if props.get(k) is not None}},
                "groups": out}

    def ask(self, question: str) -> dict:
        return self.kg.answer(question, use_llm=False).to_dict()

    def structured(self, question: str, payload: str) -> dict:
        """Check a structured answer (claims citing edges) and write the answer from it."""
        kg = self.kg
        entities = kg.link_entities(question)
        evidence = kg.retrieve(kg.classify(question, entities), entities)
        res = kg.verify_structured(payload, evidence)
        res["evidence"] = [{"edge_id": e.edge_id, "statement": e.statement} for e in evidence]
        return res

    def check(self, question: str, answer: str) -> dict:
        """Check an answer someone else wrote against the evidence for the question."""
        kg = self.kg
        entities = kg.link_entities(question)
        intent = kg.classify(question, entities)
        evidence = kg.retrieve(intent, entities)
        checks = kg.check_groundedness(answer, evidence, entities, generated=True,
                                       vocabulary=set(self.dic["index"]),
                                       linker=kg.link_entities)
        return {"intent": intent, "entities": entities, "checks": checks,
                "evidence": [{"statement": e.statement, "source_record": e.source_record,
                              "source": e.source, "url": e.url} for e in evidence[:8]],
                "evidence_count": len(evidence)}


# A trade name ChEMBL lists under a molecule it is not: T-DM1's brand under trastuzumab.
_MISFILED = {("Kadcyla", "CHEMBL1201585")}
_MARKS = "®™©℠"          # registered, trade mark, copyright, service mark


def _sig(text: str) -> str:
    """The one rule by which the relation map compares a typed name with a stored one:
    trademark signs dropped before compatibility folding (so "™" never becomes the
    letters "TM"), then case and accents ignored and spaces, punctuation and invisible
    characters dropped. Letters of any script, digits and symbols are kept, so
    "trastu+zumab", "interferon" with a Greek gamma or "trastuzumab" with a radiation sign
    are not "trastuzumab" or "interferon"."""
    t = unicodedata.normalize("NFKC", "".join(c for c in text or "" if c not in _MARKS))
    t = unicodedata.normalize("NFKD", t.casefold())
    return "".join(c for c in t if not unicodedata.combining(c)
                   and unicodedata.category(c)[0] not in "PZC")


def _add_name(into: dict, name: str, nid: str) -> None:
    if name and _sig(name):
        into.setdefault(_sig(name), {}).setdefault(nid, name)


def _number(v) -> bool:
    return isinstance(v, (int, float)) and not isinstance(v, bool) and v == v


def _obj(raw) -> dict:
    """Stored props as a dict; anything else (null, a list, broken JSON) as empty."""
    try:
        v = json.loads(raw) if raw else {}
    except ValueError:
        return {}
    return v if isinstance(v, dict) else {}


def make_handler(engine: Engine):
    class Handler(BaseHTTPRequestHandler):
        def _send(self, code: int, body: bytes, ctype: str):
            self.send_response(code)
            self.send_header("Content-Type", ctype)
            self.send_header("Content-Length", str(len(body)))
            self.end_headers()
            self.wfile.write(body)

        def do_GET(self):
            if self.path in ("/", "/index.html"):
                self._send(200, PAGE.read_bytes(), "text/html; charset=utf-8")
            else:
                self._send(404, b"not found", "text/plain")

        def do_POST(self):
            try:
                n = int(self.headers.get("Content-Length") or 0)
                body = json.loads(self.rfile.read(min(n, 20000)) or b"{}")
                text = str(body.get("text") or "").strip()
                if len(text) > 500:                      # refused, not silently cut
                    raise ValueError("input is longer than 500 characters")
                if not text:
                    raise ValueError("empty input")
                if self.path not in ("/api/resolve", "/api/ask", "/api/check",
                                     "/api/structured", "/api/explore"):
                    return self._send(404, b"{}", "application/json")
                with engine.lock:
                    if self.path == "/api/explore":
                        res = engine.explore(text)
                    elif self.path == "/api/resolve":
                        res = engine.resolve(text)
                    elif self.path == "/api/ask":
                        res = engine.ask(text)
                    elif self.path == "/api/structured":
                        answer = str(body.get("answer") or "").strip()[:20000]
                        if not answer:
                            raise ValueError("empty answer")
                        res = engine.structured(text, answer)
                    else:
                        answer = str(body.get("answer") or "").strip()[:4000]
                        if not answer:
                            raise ValueError("empty answer")
                        res = engine.check(text, answer)
                self._send(200, json.dumps(res, ensure_ascii=False,
                                           allow_nan=False).encode("utf-8"),
                           "application/json; charset=utf-8")
            except Exception as e:  # show the reason on the page, not a dead request
                self._send(400, json.dumps({"error": str(e)}, ensure_ascii=False).encode("utf-8"),
                           "application/json; charset=utf-8")

        def log_message(self, *a):
            pass
    return Handler


def main() -> None:
    port = int(sys.argv[1]) if len(sys.argv) > 1 else PORT
    engine = Engine()
    print(f"OncoKG try-it page: http://localhost:{port}", flush=True)
    ThreadingHTTPServer(("127.0.0.1", port), make_handler(engine)).serve_forever()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
# Independent relation-map review — round 32

Reviewed **HEAD `b21da2b29c3333af2075433043e32eeb2bb8af21`**, parent `4493fe0f21ad7615594be5d7715b1b90de2a1ed6`, on 2026-11-04. **CHANGES REQUIRED.** The test suite passes, and the new design prevents the historical longer-input leaks. However, the map can still draw a node for an input that is not a whole stored name, or silently choose one of several live same-name nodes. New exact stored aliases also map different products to the wrong molecule; these are reported separately as data defects.

## Verification and limits

- Ran the requested `python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider`: **361 passed**. The first run took 48.16s and emitted one Windows cp949 subprocess-reader warning. A justified clean rerun with `PYTHONUTF8=1`, bytecode disabled and pytest/temp paths under m32 produced **361 passed in 43.71s, no warnings**. [Clean output](pytest_utf8.txt), [initial output](pytest.txt). The initial pytest invocation used pytest's default temporary location; subsequent audit scripts, artifacts and final-run temporary files are under m32.
- Started the actual application with **`python src/app.py 8932`** and exercised it over HTTP. The served page exactly equalled `web/try.html`. Root independently captured **50 requests**, including the principal code/data counterexamples, valid ids, refusals and input errors; other lanes retain their own original-CLI captures. [First30](root_http.json), [additional20 plus stored/live-name checks](root_additional_http.json). HTTP200 alone was never treated as semantic success.
- Compared **all 6,469 live centers** against independent SQLite results: **zero errors**, covering 22 relation/direction/type/role combinations. **19 DB assertions and 26 UI/server assertions pass**. All 36 demo HTTP responses equal direct Engine results and the query-only HEAD^ comparison.
- UI checks execute the actual served JavaScript in a **Node VM DOM/fetch harness**, using real HTTP for choices and demos. They verify generated markup, delegated clicks and state transitions; they are **not a painted-browser or visual-layout test**.
- No production code was changed and no model was called. Read paths and `use_llm=False` were inspected; private data mutations use in-memory copies. Final tracked status is clean; all 67 files in the root HTTP baseline, including the database/dictionary, remain byte-identical. That baseline was taken after the initial test run, not before it. The owned port 8932 is closed. [Root HTTP hash check](root_confirmation.json), [final integrity](integrity_final.json), [diff under review](review_diff.txt). The conditional KOA Studio/KhOpenApi before-and-after code requirement is not triggered by this read-only review.

## Code blockers

**M32-B1 — comparing an echoed linker window does not prove a stored-name match.** At `src/app.py:111-115`, `_sig(input)` is compared with `_sig(matched_text)`. But `src/query.py:872-877` supplies the candidate input window after looking it up through the lossy canonical key (`src/textrules.py:340-347`). This proves input-span coverage, not equality to an actual stored alias. `_plain` permits characters the dictionary discards.

`trastu+zumab`, `trastu$zumab`, and `trastu=zumab` all draw **CHEMBL1201585 TRASTUZUMAB**. So do `trastuzumabß`, `trastuzumabø`, `trastuzumabæ`, `trastuzumabł`, `trastuzumabð`, and `trastuzumabı`. The significant extra symbol/letter is absent from every stored alias of the drawn drug. The root independently checked full stored aliases/live nodes for `trastu+zumab` and `trastuzumabß`; neither has a matching name. This violates P32-1 regardless of whether the altered text denotes a real marketed product. [HTTP and alias proof](root_additional_http.json), [broader variants](attacks/http_identity.json), [independent sanity assertions](attacks/sanity_assertions.json).

**M32-B2 — company fallback bypasses significant-name equality.** `src/app.py:120-122` accepts a canonical company key whenever `_plain(text)` is true. It never compares the input with a stored name/key retaining significant symbols. **`AstraZeneca +` -> ORG:astrazeneca**, **`Merck + Co` -> ORG:merckco**, and **`Pfizer$`/`Pfi+zer` -> ORG:pfizer**. `Roche $` draws only the company, whereas `Roche` correctly offers the folic-acid/company choice. This is an independent acceptance path even when the linker-window equality fails. The requested `_latin` implementation is absent from actual HEAD; the reviewed implementation is `_plain`. `Merck & Co` itself is a valid stored-label control, not a reported counterexample. [Independent HTTP](root_http.json), [extra company controls](attacks/http_identity.json).

**M32-B3 — shared-name collection uses inconsistent equivalence rules.** P32-3 requires a reader choice for several live nodes sharing one name, ignoring case/spacing/punctuation. The map's `_label_key` retains punctuation, target fallback is literal lowercase, and drug candidates inherit P32-5's question-only preferred-name selection (`src/app.py:111-125,242-244`; `src/query.py:742-747`). A sweep of155 ambiguous spellings finds six that draw rather than choose:

| Exact input | Live candidates sharing the significant name | Incorrect automatic result |
|---|---|---|
| `ABT-510` / `ABT 510` | CHEMBL386115 and CHEMBL262489 | Each spelling draws its own preferred-name node |
| `MAGE-A3` | CHEMBL2108705 ASTUPROTIMUT-R and ENSG00000221867 MAGEA3 | Draws the drug; `MAGEA3` correctly chooses |
| `ctla-4` | CHEMBL1789844 IPILIMUMAB and ENSG00000163599 CTLA4 | Draws the drug; `CTLA4` correctly chooses |
| `FLUDEOXYGLUCOSE F 18` / `FLUDEOXYGLUCOSE-F18` | CHEMBL2030434 and CHEMBL497613 | Each spelling draws its own preferred-name node |

All candidates were independently checked live in SQL. MAGE-A3 and ctla-4 demonstrate the failure without relying on the drug-preferred-name exception. Duplicate source names are data facts; automatically selecting one is a map-code violation. P32-5 allows preferred-name selection in question linking, but does not waive the map's choice rule. [Complete sweep](attacks/shared_signatures.json), [independent HTTP/SQL confirmation](root_additional_http.json).

**M32-B4 — a supposedly ignored trademark changes identity.** NFKC expands `™` into letters `TM` before `_sig` removes trademark characters (`src/app.py:237-239`). **`Tarceva`/`Tarceva®` draw CHEMBL1079742 ERLOTINIB HYDROCHLORIDE; `Tarceva™` draws CHEMBL553 ERLOTINIB.** The latter uses a separate stored AACT alias `tarcevatm`. That alias explains the lookup but cannot authorize interpreting a literal ignored trademark as significant letters under P32-1. `trastuzumab™` and `AstraZeneca™` instead lose recall; those are should-fix manifestations, not additional wrong-node cases. [Root HTTP](root_http.json), [interpretation checks](attacks/sanity_assertions.json).

A repair needs to establish identity against actual stored names, collect **all live candidates under one consistent name-equivalence rule**, and only then draw or choose. Comparing text with its own extracted window or checking a character class cannot establish this. Trademark handling must precede compatibility expansion. These are review conclusions, not an implemented patch.

## Data blockers, separate from code

The following are **exact stored AACT aliases** in `data/processed/resolved.json:1`, JSON path `dictionary.index[canonical_key(input)]`. The new map accepts the whole alias faithfully, but its source assignment identifies the wrong substance/product. These are outside the explicitly waived bevacizumab biosimilar codes, 13-cis-retinoic-acid and Roche limitations.

| Finding | Exact input and observed center | Independent identity evidence |
|---|---|---|
| M32-D1 | `bupivacaine hydrochloride` -> **ANTIMONY / CHEMBL4297652** | NCI identifies an amide local anesthetic, not antimony. [NCI definition](https://www.cancer.gov/publications/dictionaries/cancer-drug/def/bupivacaine-hydrochloride). |
| M32-D2 | `irinotecan liposome`, `irinotecan liposome injection`, `nanoliposomal irinotecan` -> **IRINOTECAN / CHEMBL481**; `doxorubicin hydrochloride liposome` -> **ordinary hydrochloride / CHEMBL359744** | These denote liposomal formulations. The Onivyde label explicitly distinguishes it from other irinotecan products; NCI separately identifies liposomal doxorubicin. [Manufacturer label](https://dailymed.nlm.nih.gov/dailymed/fda/fdaDrugXsl.cfm?setid=c8b58efa-1820-48a4-b70d-62918fc4abfc), [NCI formulation](https://www.cancer.gov/about-cancer/treatment/drugs/doxorubicin-hydrochloride-liposome). |
| M32-D3 | `ropeginterferon alfa-2b` -> **INTERFERON ALFA-2B / CHEMBL1201558** | NCI describes a PEG conjugate, rather than unconjugated interferon alfa-2b. [NCI definition](https://www.cancer.gov/publications/dictionaries/cancer-drug/def/ropeginterferon-alfa-2b). |

Root independently reproduced every table row over HTTP and retained the matching source records and live center. `Onivyde`, `liposomal irinotecan`, `Doxil`, and `liposomal doxorubicin` are refusal controls, showing that another stored spelling of a distinct formulation is drawn. [Root captures](root_additional_http.json), [source records and controls](attacks/http_data_aliases.json), [full analysis](attacks/FINDINGS.md). The 317 candidate aliases in the exploratory screen are **not 317 proven defects**. These selected rows need source curation; another free-text word-list patch would not establish source identity.

# Codex verification brief — relation map on the try-it page (commit bfcb83d)

You are an independent reviewer. Do NOT edit any tracked file. Write scratch files only under
`audit_evidence_claims/m1/`. Put your report in `audit_evidence_claims/m1/REPORT.md`.
End with exactly one line: `VERDICT: APPROVE` or `VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED`.

## What was added

`src/app.py` `Engine.explore(text, per_group=30)` and `POST /api/explore`; `web/try.html` section "0. 관계 지도"
(`drawMap`, `explore`, `itemDetail`, the GKO/TKO/ACT/PH tables, click-through and the "따라온 길" trail);
`tests/test_app.py::test_explore_draws_a_drug_and_refuses_a_biosimilar`. Run tests with
`python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` (317). Start the page with `python src/app.py [port]` (use a port
other than 8765, which may already be in use) and exercise it over HTTP.

The page is for a domain reviewer to look at one node (drug, target, company, investigator, trial, disease) and
everything a live edge ties it to, grouped by relation, and to click through. It is read-only.

## The guarantees claimed (page text in section 0 and the `explore` docstring)

1. Every neighbour shown is joined to the centre by a live (not retired) edge to a live node, and the group label
   states the relation and its direction correctly (e.g. "이 약의 임상을 주관한 곳 (주관 ≠ 개발)" for
   Company -SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF-> Drug seen from the drug).
2. Details shown for a neighbour are the stored values: trial phases/status/start/title/url, sponsor trial count
   (`evidence_trials`) and sponsor class, drug trial count in the graph (EVALUATES), max phase, action type.
3. A name the question linker refuses (biosimilar, distinct product, tracer) is reported as refused, never drawn
   as the originator molecule; an unknown name says "not found".
4. Groups are ordered by size and each list keeps the `per_group` neighbours with most evidence; the page says
   when a list is cut and shows the true total.
5. Nothing is written; no model is called.

## What to verify (reproduce; cite file:line and exact inputs)

A. **Wrong relations or directions.** Any centre where an edge is grouped under the wrong label or direction,
   self-loops (src == dst), edges with both ends equal to the centre, PARENT_OF/SPONSORS/STUDIES/ASSOCIATED_WITH
   direction wording, Investigator vs Company handling, nodes whose type the tables do not cover.
B. **Name resolution.** `explore` takes the first non-refused hit of `link_entities`. Inputs with several entities
   ("osimertinib EGFR"), a refused token next to a real one ("Mvasi bevacizumab"), salts vs parents, company aliases
   with `also_matching_ids` (only the first id is drawn — is that misleading?), node ids vs names that collide,
   case/whitespace/Unicode, long inputs. Is anything drawn that the input does not name, or a refused product drawn
   as the originator?
C. **Wrong values.** Trial counts, sponsor counts, max phase and action shown versus the database
   (`data/processed/oncokg.sqlite`); retired edges/nodes leaking in; counts computed over retired edges;
   the `trials` count chunking; JSON props that are null or malformed.
D. **Cutting and ordering.** Is the "most evidence" ordering what the page says (trials are ordered by start date
   string — is that what the text implies)? Does the map's subset (`drawMap` allocation) ever hide a whole group
   or misstate totals? The Disease node (thousands of neighbours) — performance and output size.
E. **Page safety and correctness.** HTML/SVG injection from labels, titles, ids, URLs (`href` with `javascript:`?),
   edge ids in `data-id` attributes, the trail/back buttons after errors, double clicks, refused/not-found states,
   dark mode legibility is not in scope.
F. **Server.** Error handling for empty/oversized input, concurrency with the lock, behaviour when the DB has
   retired rows.
G. **Docs and wording.** Section 0 text, README/README.ko lines for `app.py`, the module docstring: do they claim
   more than the code does? Korean labels in GKO/ACT/PH that misstate the stored code.

## Approval bar

No known way for the map to show a relation that is not a live edge, a wrong direction or relation wording, a
value that differs from the stored one, a refused product drawn as the originator, or script injection. Report
should-fix items (misleading wording, cut lists without notice) separately from blockers.

## Before round 32 — the map takes one whole name (design change, not a word-list repair)

Rounds 20 to 31 each found new wording that the question linker's markers did not cover
(a phrase between the joining word and the partner, dye names outside the list). A list of
words cannot be complete, so the map no longer depends on it:

| # | Change | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| P32-1 | `explore` draws only when the whole input is one name: the linker reads exactly one entity and its matched text has the same letters, digits and symbols (any script; case, spaces, punctuation, trademark signs aside) as the input. Anything more returns `not_one_name` and the page asks for the name alone | a word the linker does not know can make the name a different product; now an unknown word means "not drawn", whatever it is |
| P32-2 | any refused part refuses the whole input (before, "osimertinib, Mvasi" drew osimertinib and listed Mvasi) | one input, one name |
| P32-3 | a name stored for several nodes is not drawn; the nodes are listed and the reader picks one by id ("Roche": folic acid alias and the company) | before, the first was drawn and the others listed |
| P32-4 | lower-case ids (nct..., chembl..., ensg...) and company names the linker cuts at "&" ("Merck & Co") are read whole | recall for one-name input |
| P32-5 | the question linker no longer decides a key shared by several drugs by its spelling, and does not fall back to a shorter name inside such a key | found by a sweep of the shared keys the earlier sweeps skipped: "medi4736" was read as atezolizumab, "5-fluorouracil" as capecitabine, "ak112" as envafolimab, "peginterferon alfa-2b" as alfa-2a, "crlx101" as camptothecin, "PD-1 mAb" as pembrolizumab, "Dha paclitaxel" as paclitaxel |

The question boxes still use the linker's markers on free text; that limit is now README
defect 15. Corpus check of the question linker (9,746 intervention strings and demo
questions): 20 change, all names whose key is shared by several drugs, now unread instead of
read as one of them. Tests 361.

## Round 32 (audit_evidence_claims/m32) — CHANGES REQUIRED (four code blockers, three data blockers)

| # | Finding | Repair |
| --- | --- | --- |
| M32-B1 | the input was compared with the linker's own window, not a stored name: "trastu+zumab", "trastuzumabß" drew trastuzumab | the map keeps its own index of stored names, keyed by one signature (`_sig`), and draws only a node a stored name of which equals the input |
| M32-B2 | the company key path took "AstraZeneca +", "Pfi+zer", "Merck + Co" | company and disease keys (letters and digits only) match only input whose signature equals its dictionary key, so a dropped symbol blocks them |
| M32-B3 | different equivalence rules per source: "MAGE-A3", "ctla-4", "ABT-510"/"ABT 510", the two FDG records drew one node | every name source goes through the same signature; several live nodes under one signature always ask the reader to choose |
| M32-B4 | NFKC turned "™" into letters before it was dropped: "Tarceva™" drew a different record | trademark signs are dropped before compatibility folding |
| M32-D1..D3 | registry (AACT) synonyms with wrong identities: bupivacaine hydrochloride under antimony; irinotecan liposome, nanoliposomal irinotecan, doxorubicin hydrochloride liposome under the plain molecule; ropeginterferon alfa-2b under interferon alfa-2b | the map reads drugs only by ChEMBL names (preferred name, synonyms, trade names), node labels and target symbols; a name found only among registry synonyms returns `registry_name` and the page says why. This also draws the right molecule for keys a registry synonym had made ambiguous (MEDI4736 durvalumab, AK112 ivonescimab, CRLX101 davamotecan pegadexamer, DHA-paclitaxel) |
| should-fix | "What trials evaluate NICOTINE?" had no entity (inherited) | in a question, a key several drugs share is read as the drug whose own preferred name it is, letter for letter |

| pre-33 | the ChEMBL trade names and biologic synonyms were reviewed before round 33: European biosimilar brands (Inflectra, Remsima, Flixabi, Equidacent, Lextemy, Herwenda, filgrastim/epoetin/pegfilgrastim/teriparatide brands) and formulations (Myocet, Fyarro, Megace ES, Jelmyto, Locteron) are filed under the reference molecule; an antibody's or protein's ChEMBL synonyms mix originator codes with biosimilar codes and brands (CT-P13, Zessly, Avsola under infliximab; ABP 980, HLX-02 under trastuzumab; Jubbonti, Wyost under denosumab) | those trade names are refused by the question linker and the map (`_OTHER_PRODUCT_NAMES`); the map does not read an antibody's or protein's ChEMBL synonyms (reply `biologic_synonym`); Kadcyla, filed under trastuzumab too, is read only as trastuzumab emtansine |

Known recall limits (fail closed): registry-only names ("IL-2", "9-ac", "5-fluorouracil", "interferon α-2b") are not drawn; two tracer drugs are refused by their own labels. Corpus check of the question linker: Equidacent and INFLECTRA® are now refused; nothing else changed. Antibody development codes (MK-3475, MEDI4736) are not drawn by the map. Tests 362.

def _number(v) -> bool:
    return isinstance(v, (int, float)) and not isinstance(v, bool) and v == v


def _obj(raw) -> dict:
    """Stored props as a dict; anything else (null, a list, broken JSON) as empty."""
    try:
        v = json.loads(raw) if raw else {}
    except ValueError:
        return {}
    return v if isinstance(v, dict) else {}


def make_handler(engine: Engine):
    class Handler(BaseHTTPRequestHandler):
        def _send(self, code: int, body: bytes, ctype: str):
            self.send_response(code)
            self.send_header("Content-Type", ctype)
            self.send_header("Content-Length", str(len(body)))
            self.end_headers()
            self.wfile.write(body)

        def do_GET(self):
            if self.path in ("/", "/index.html"):
                self._send(200, PAGE.read_bytes(), "text/html; charset=utf-8")
            else:
                self._send(404, b"not found", "text/plain")

        def do_POST(self):
            try:
                n = int(self.headers.get("Content-Length") or 0)
                body = json.loads(self.rfile.read(min(n, 20000)) or b"{}")
                text = str(body.get("text") or "").strip()
                if len(text) > 500:                      # refused, not silently cut
                    raise ValueError("input is longer than 500 characters")
                if not text:
                    raise ValueError("empty input")
                if self.path not in ("/api/resolve", "/api/ask", "/api/check",
                                     "/api/structured", "/api/explore"):
                    return self._send(404, b"{}", "application/json")
                with engine.lock:
                    if self.path == "/api/explore":
                        res = engine.explore(text)
                    elif self.path == "/api/resolve":
                        res = engine.resolve(text)
                    elif self.path == "/api/ask":
                        res = engine.ask(text)
                    elif self.path == "/api/structured":
                        answer = str(body.get("answer") or "").strip()[:20000]
                        if not answer:
                            raise ValueError("empty answer")
                        res = engine.structured(text, answer)
                    else:
                        answer = str(body.get("answer") or "").strip()[:4000]
                        if not answer:
                            raise ValueError("empty answer")
                        res = engine.check(text, answer)
                self._send(200, json.dumps(res, ensure_ascii=False,
                                           allow_nan=False).encode("utf-8"),
                           "application/json; charset=utf-8")
            except Exception as e:  # show the reason on the page, not a dead request
                self._send(400, json.dumps({"error": str(e)}, ensure_ascii=False).encode("utf-8"),
                           "application/json; charset=utf-8")

        def log_message(self, *a):
            pass
    return Handler


def main() -> None:
    port = int(sys.argv[1]) if len(sys.argv) > 1 else PORT
    engine = Engine()
    print(f"OncoKG try-it page: http://localhost:{port}", flush=True)
    ThreadingHTTPServer(("127.0.0.1", port), make_handler(engine)).serve_forever()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()


HTTP prior harness
"""Independent HTTP corroboration; production is read-only, outputs stay in m32."""
import hashlib
import json
import os
from pathlib import Path
import subprocess
import time
import urllib.error
import urllib.request

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
BASE = 'http://127.0.0.1:8932'

def digest(path):
    h = hashlib.sha256()
    with path.open('rb') as f:
        for block in iter(lambda: f.read(1024 * 1024), b''):
            h.update(block)
    return h.hexdigest()

def snapshot():
    tracked = subprocess.check_output(['git', 'ls-files', '-z'], cwd=ROOT).decode().split('\0')
    paths = [p for p in tracked if p and not p.startswith('audit_evidence')]
    paths += ['data/processed/oncokg.sqlite', 'data/processed/resolved.json']
    return {p: digest(ROOT / p) for p in sorted(set(paths)) if (ROOT / p).is_file()}

def post(text, route='/api/explore', **extra):
    data = json.dumps({'text': text, **extra}).encode()
    req = urllib.request.Request(BASE + route, data=data, headers={'Content-Type': 'application/json'})
    start = time.perf_counter()
    try:
        with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=90) as response:
            status, body = response.status, response.read()
    except urllib.error.HTTPError as e:
        status, body = e.code, e.read()
    return {'text': text, 'route': route, 'status': status,
            'seconds': time.perf_counter() - start, 'body': json.loads(body)}

def main():
    before = snapshot()
    (OUT / 'root_before_hashes.json').write_text(json.dumps(before, indent=2), encoding='utf-8')
    page = urllib.request.urlopen(BASE + '/', timeout=30).read()
    page_exact = page == (ROOT / 'web/try.html').read_bytes()
    cases = [
        'trastuzumab', 'trastu$zumab', 'trastu+zumab', 'trastu=zumab',
        'AstraZeneca', 'AstraZeneca +', 'Merck & Co', 'Merck + Co', 'Roche', 'Roche $',
        'Tarceva', 'Tarceva™', 'Tarceva®', 'Tarceva©',
        'osimertinib, Mvasi', 'osimertinib EGFR',
        'trastuzumab linked at lysine residues to eFluor 450',
        'trastuzumab emtansine conjugated at lysine residues to eFluor 450',
        'b.i.o.similar bevacizumab', 'lip.osomal doxorubicin',
        'medi4736', '5-fluorouracil', 'DHA paclitaxel', 'EGFR',
        'chembl1201585', 'ensg00000146648', 'nct00000105',
        'CHEMBL1201585 +', '', 'x' * 501,
    ]
    result = [post(t) for t in cases]
    (OUT / 'root_http.json').write_text(json.dumps(result, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2), encoding='utf-8')
    after = snapshot()
    report = {'page_equals_worktree': page_exact, 'request_count': len(result),
              'changed_files': [p for p in before if before[p] != after.get(p)],
              'hashes_after': after,
              'cases': [{'text': r['text'], 'http': r['status'],
                         'center': r['body'].get('center', {}).get('id'),
                         'result_keys': list(r['body'])} for r in result]}
    (OUT / 'root_confirmation.json').write_text(json.dumps(report, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2), encoding='utf-8')
    print(json.dumps({k: v for k, v in report.items() if k != 'hashes_after'}, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2))

if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()

+"""Independent round-33 HTTP confirmation. No production writes or model calls."""
+import json
+import sys
+import time
+import unicodedata
+import urllib.error
+import urllib.request
+from pathlib import Path
+
+ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
+OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
+BASE = 'http://127.0.0.1:8933'
+sys.path.insert(0, str(ROOT / 'src'))
+from app import _sig
+
+
+def post(text, route='/api/explore', **extra):
+    req = urllib.request.Request(BASE + route,
+        data=json.dumps({'text': text, **extra}).encode(),
+        headers={'Content-Type': 'application/json'})
+    start = time.perf_counter()
+    try:
+        with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=120) as response:
+            status, body = response.status, response.read()
+    except urllib.error.HTTPError as e:
+        status, body = e.code, e.read()
+    return {'text': text, 'route': route, 'status': status,
+            'seconds': time.perf_counter() - start, 'body': json.loads(body)}
+
+
+def main():
+    cases = [
+        'trastu+zumab', 'trastu$zumab', 'trastu=zumab', 'trastuzumabß',
+        'trastuzumabγ', 'trastuzumab☢', 'trastuzumab²', 'AstraZeneca +',
+        'Merck + Co', 'Pfizer$', 'Pfi+zer', 'Roche $', 'Merck & Co',
+        'ABT-510', 'ABT 510', 'MAGE-A3', 'MAGEA3', 'ctla-4', 'CTLA4',
+        'FLUDEOXYGLUCOSE F 18', 'FLUDEOXYGLUCOSE-F18', 'Tarceva',
+        'Tarceva™', 'Tarceva®', 'Tarceva©', 'Tarceva℠',
+        'bupivacaine hydrochloride', 'irinotecan liposome',
+        'irinotecan liposome injection', 'nanoliposomal irinotecan',
+        'doxorubicin hydrochloride liposome', 'ropeginterferon alfa-2b',
+        '13-cis retinoic acid', 'thymosin α1', 'ifn-α-2b', 'interferon α-2b',
+        'trastuzumab™', 'AstraZeneca™', 'CT-P13', 'ABP 980', 'Zessly',
+        'MK-3475', 'MEDI4736', 'Roche', 'Kadcyla',
+        'Myocet', 'Myocet™', 'My/o/cet', 'My ocet',
+        'Inflectra', 'Inf/lectra', 'Equidacent', 'Equi/dacent',
+        'Fyarro', 'Fy/arro', 'Megace ES', 'Megace/ES',
+        'Oraqix', 'Actikerall', 'Betnesol-n', 'Depakote',
+        'osimertinib, Mvasi', 'osimertinib EGFR',
+        'chembl1201585', 'ensg00000146648', 'nct00200395',
+        'CHEMBL1201585 +', 'ＣＨＥＭＢＬ１２０１５８５',
+        'trastuzu\u200bmab', 'trastuzu\u200dmab', 'trastu\u202ezumab',
+        'trastuzumab\ufe0f', '', 'x' * 501,
+    ]
+    rows = [post(t) for t in cases]
+    rows += [post('What trials evaluate NICOTINE?', r,
+                  **({'answer': 'Nicotine is evaluated in trials.'} if r != '/api/ask' else {}))
+             for r in ['/api/ask', '/api/structured', '/api/check']]
+    (OUT / 'root_http.json').write_text(json.dumps(rows, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2), encoding='utf-8')
+    summary = [{'text': r['text'], 'status': r['status'],
+        'center': r['body'].get('center', {}).get('id'),
+        'keys': list(r['body']),
+        'choose_ids': [c['id'] for c in r['body'].get('choose', [])]}
+        for r in rows]
+    result = {'request_count': len(rows),
+        'page_equals_worktree': urllib.request.urlopen(BASE).read() == (ROOT / 'web/try.html').read_bytes(),
+        'signature_controls': [{'original': t, 'variant': v, 'sig_original': _sig(t),
+            'sig_variant': _sig(v), 'same': _sig(t) == _sig(v)}
+            for t, v in [('Myocet','Myocet™'), ('Myocet','My/o/cet'),
+                         ('Inflectra','Inf/lectra'), ('Equidacent','Equi/dacent')]],
+        'cases': summary,
+        'browser': {'rendered_test_completed': False,
+            'reason': 'CUA createBrowserTab timed out; subsequent getState returned no apps/browsers, native pipe unavailable, browser access host unavailable.'}}
+    (OUT / 'root_confirmation.json').write_text(json.dumps(result, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2), encoding='utf-8')
+    print(json.dumps(result, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2))
+
+
+if __name__ == '__main__':
+    main()

              "trastuzumab linked to Cy3", "trastuzumab linked to TAMRA",
              "trastuzumab emtansine linked to Alexa Fluor 488",
              "trastuzumab emtansine conjugated to polyethylene glycol"):
        assert e.explore(t).get("refused"), t
    for t, label in (("trastuzumab not radiolabelled", "TRASTUZUMAB"),
                     ("trastuzumab deruxtecan antibody-drug conjugate", "TRASTUZUMAB DERUXTECAN")):
        assert _page(e, t)["center"]["label"] == label, t

# Codex review m31
def test_setting_phrase_before_partner_and_gene_relations_and_question_scaffold():
    import app
    e = app.Engine()
    for t in ("trastuzumab linked at lysine residues to eFluor 450",
              "trastuzumab emtansine conjugated in solution to eFluor 450",
              "trastuzumab deruxtecan functionalized in solution with eFluor 450"):
